```text
    "text": "import { eq, sql } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { db } from \"@/server/db\";\nimport { workers } from \"@/server/db/schema\";\n\nconst workerTurnChains = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\nconst conversationMutationChains = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\nconst backgroundTasks = new Set<Promise<void>>();\nconst backgroundTasksByRunId = new Map<string, Set<Promise<void>>>();\nconst conversationDeletionRequests = new Set<string>();\nconst completedConversationDeletionRequests = new Set<string>();\n\nexport function isWorkerTurnSupersededError(error: unknown): boolean {\n  const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n  return /\\bworker turn (?:was )?superseded by a newer worker turn\\b/i.test(message);\n}\n\nfunction runOnChain<T>(\n  chains: Map<string, Promise<void>>,\n  key: string,\n  task: () => Promise<T>,\n): Promise<T> {\n  const previous = chains.get(key) ?? Promise.resolve();\n  const next = previous.then(() => task());\n  const tracked = next.then(() => undefined, () => undefined);\n  chains.set(key, tracked);\n  return next.finally(() => {\n    if (chains.get(key) === tracked) {\n      chains.delete(key);\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nexport function runWorkerTurn<T>(workerId: string, task: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  return runOnChain(workerTurnChains, workerId, task);\n}\n\n/**\n * Read the worker's current turn fence value. Returns 0 when the worker\n * row is missing (treated as the default generation).\n */\nexport async function readWorkerTurnGeneration(workerId: string): Promise<number> {\n  const record = await db\n    .select({ turnGeneration: workers.turnGeneration })\n    .from(workers)\n    .where(eq(workers.id, workerId))\n    .get();\n  return record?.turnGeneration ?? 0;\n}\n\n/**\n * Capture a worker turn generation when the worker exists. A null result means\n * the bridge request is not backed by a persisted conversation worker and\n * therefore cannot participate in the database turn fence.\n */\nexport async function captureWorkerTurnGeneration(workerId: string): Promise<number | null> {\n  const record = await db\n    .select({ turnGeneration: workers.turnGeneration })\n    .from(workers)\n    .where(eq(workers.id, workerId))\n    .get();\n  return record?.turnGeneration ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Atomically advance the worker turn fence and (optionally) reset persisted\n * worker state into a delivery-safe shape in the SAME mutation, so an\n * immediate queued delivery cannot observe stale `working` state after a\n * successful cancel. Returns the new generation value.\n *\n * Callers capture the returned generation for the new delivery; any older\n * in-flight completion that captured a smaller value must refuse to persist\n * terminal updates (see `isWorkerTurnGenerationCurrent`).\n */\nexport async function advanceWorkerTurnGeneration(\n  workerId: string,\n  reset?: { status?: string; clearCurrentText?: boolean; updatedAt?: Date },\n): Promise<number> {\n  await db.update(workers).set({\n    turnGeneration: sql`${workers.turnGeneration} + 1`,\n    ...(reset?.status !== undefined ? { status: reset.status } : {}),\n    ...(reset?.clearCurrentText ? { currentText: \"\" } : {}),\n    ...(reset?.updatedAt ? { updatedAt: reset.updatedAt } : {}),\n  }).where(eq(workers.id, workerId));\n  return readWorkerTurnGeneration(workerId);\n}\n\n/**\n * True when `capturedGeneration` still matches the worker's persisted fence,\n * i.e. no newer interrupt delivery has superseded the captured turn. A missing\n * worker row reports `false` so stale callers stop persisting.\n */\nexport async function isWorkerTurnGenerationCurrent(\n  workerId: string,\n  capturedGeneration: number,\n): Promise<boolean> {\n  const record = await db\n    .select({ turnGeneration: workers.turnGeneration })\n    .from(workers)\n    .where(eq(workers.id, workerId))\n    .get();\n  if (!record) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  return record.turnGeneration === capturedGeneration;\n}\n\nexport function runConversationMutation<T>(runId: string, task: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  return runOnChain(conversationMutationChains, runId, task);\n}\n\nexport function trackConversationBackgroundTask<T>(\n  task: Promise<T>,\n  options: { runId?: string } = {},\n): Promise<T> {\n  const runId = options.runId?.trim() || null;\n  const tracked = task.then(() => undefined, () => undefined);\n  backgroundTasks.add(tracked);\n  if (runId) {\n    const runTasks = backgroundTasksByRunId.get(runId) ?? new Set<Promise<void>>();\n    runTasks.add(tracked);\n    backgroundTasksByRunId.set(runId, runTasks);\n  }\n  void tracked.finally(() => {\n    backgroundTasks.delete(tracked);\n    if (runId) {\n      const runTasks = backgroundTasksByRunId.get(runId);\n      runTasks?.delete(tracked);\n      if (!runTasks || runTasks.size === 0) {\n        backgroundTasksByRunId.delete(runId);\n      }\n      if (completedConversationDeletionRequests.has(runId) && !backgroundTasksByRunId.has(runId)) {\n        completedConversationDeletionRequests.delete(runId);\n        conversationDeletionRequests.delete(runId);\n      }\n    }\n  });\n  return task;\n}\n\nexport function requestConversationDeletion(runId: string): void {\n  completedConversationDeletionRequests.delete(runId);\n  conversationDeletionRequests.add(runId);\n}\n\nexport function isConversationDeletionRequested(runId: string): boolean {\n  return conversationDeletionRequests.has(runId);\n}\n\nexport function completeConversationDeletion(runId: string): void {\n  if (!backgroundTasksByRunId.has(runId)) {\n    conversationDeletionRequests.delete(runId);\n    completedConversationDeletionRequests.delete(runId);\n    return;\n  }\n  completedConversationDeletionRequests.add(runId);\n}\n\nexport async function waitForConversationBackgroundTasks(\n  runId: string,\n  timeoutMs = 30_000,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;\n  while ((backgroundTasksByRunId.get(runId)?.size ?? 0) > 0) {\n    const remaining = deadline - Date.now();\n    const runTasks = Array.from(backgroundTasksByRunId.get(runId) ?? []);\n    if (remaining <= 0) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `Timed out waiting for ${runTasks.length} background task(s) for conversation ${runId}`,\n      );\n    }\n    await Promise.race([\n      Promise.all(runTasks),\n      new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(remaining, 25))),\n    ]);\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function waitForConversationBackgroundTasksForTests(timeoutMs = 1_000): Promise<void> {\n  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;\n  while (backgroundTasks.size > 0) {\n    const remaining = deadline - Date.now();\n    if (remaining <= 0) {\n      throw new Error(`Timed out waiting for ${backgroundTasks.size} conversation background task(s)`);\n    }\n    await Promise.race([\n      Promise.all(Array.from(backgroundTasks)),\n      new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(remaining, 25))),\n    ]);\n  }\n}\n\nexport function __resetWorkerTurnChainsForTests() {\n  workerTurnChains.clear();\n  conversationMutationChains.clear();\n  backgroundTasks.clear();\n  backgroundTasksByRunId.clear();\n  conversationDeletionRequests.clear();\n  completedConversationDeletionRequests.clear();\n}\n\n  await db.insert(messages).values(userMessage);\n  await db.update(runs).set({\n    status: isPlanningRun(run) ? That row is\n      // an orphan — a torn-down turn that never got its terminal row — and\n      // left alone it would exempt this worker from the watchdog forever\n      // while telling every UI surface a dead question is still answerable.\n      // Close it, then carry on reaping.\n      if (liveAgent && streamHasOpenHumanInput(entries)) {\n        const closed = await closeStaleHumanInputEntries({\n          workerId: worker.id,\n          kind: \"elicitation\",\n          reason: \"the worker is no longer waiting for an answer\",\n        }) + await closeStaleHumanInputEntries({\n          workerId: worker.id,\n          kind: \"permission\",\n          reason: \"the worker is no longer waiting for a decision\",\n        });\n        if (closed > 0) {\n          process.stderr.write(\n            `[stuck-reaper] worker ${worker.id} had ${closed} orphaned human-input row(s) the bridge no longer holds; closed them\\n`,\n          );\n          notifyEventStreamSubscribers();\n        }\n      }\n\n      const liveAgentState = normalizeStatus(liveAgent?.state);\n      const liveAgentQuiesced = Boolean(liveAgent)\n        && liveAgentState !== \"error\"\n        && !isActiveAgentState(liveAgentState);\n      const liveAgentUpdatedAtMs = readAgentUpdatedAtMs(liveAgent);\n      const liveAgentFinishedAfterLastEntry = liveAgentQuiesced\n        && (liveAgentUpdatedAtMs !== null\n          ? liveAgentUpdatedAtMs > lastEntryTs\n          : Boolean((liveAgent?.stopReason ?? \"\").trim()));\n\n      if (liveAgent && liveAgentFinishedAfterLastEntry) {\n        process.stderr.write(\n          `[stuck-reaper] worker ${worker.id} already finished its turn in the bridge; ` +\n          `reconciling the lost completion instead of re-delivering\\n`,\n"
```

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"progress","timestamp":"2026-08-02T20:20:43.353Z","phase":"commentary"} -->
## Codex progress · 2026-08-02T20:20:43.353Z

The new regression now passes: after a missing direct Claude worker is reattached in an idle state, OmniHarness actively sends a recovery continuation and waits for that recovered turn in the background; it no longer marks the conversation “done” and waits for you to wake it. I’m checking the adjacent recovery cases now—especially completed turns and genuine “waiting for user” states—so the fix doesn’t create duplicate work.

<!-- /codex-event -->

## Codex progress · 2026-08-02T20:22:48.491Z

All lifecycle tests passed, including the real subprocess restart scenario: 24 files, 33 tests. The suite still prints an unrelated existing warning about an incomplete auto-commit mock, but it does not fail any tests and is outside this fix.

This was a real lifecycle design bug, so I’m using the project’s bug-learning guide now to record the exact lesson: reattaching an idle provider session is not the same as resuming an interrupted turn.

````text
    "text": "---\nname: learning-from-bugs\ndescription: Use when a meaningful bug, regression, architecture flaw, storage issue, memory problem, persistence failure, control-plane failure, or system-level failure has been diagnosed or fixed\n---\n\n# Learning From Bugs\n\nWhen a bug teaches something reusable, capture the lesson before moving on.\n\n## Use This For\n\nUse this after diagnosing or fixing bugs that involve:\n\n- architecture or ownership boundaries,\n- storage, persistence, migrations, caching, or file paths,\n- memory management, leaks, lifecycle, cleanup, or resource pressure,\n- concurrency, queues, retries, resumability, or recovery,\n- control planes, event streams, background workers, or long-running jobs,\n- security, data loss, user trust, or incident-like failures,\n- any bug that took repeated attempts or revealed a wrong mental model.\n\nSkip it for typos, obvious one-line UI mistakes, throwaway test fixture fixes, or purely local churn that will not help future work.\n\n## Required Output\n\nWrite a short learning note to:\n\n`docs/superpowers/learnings/YYYY-MM-DD-<bug-or-lesson-slug>.md`\n\nCreate the directory if needed.\n\nUse this shape:\n\n```markdown\n# [Lesson Title]\n\n**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD\n**Context:** [project, feature, or subsystem]\n**Symptom:** [what failed]\n**Root Cause:** [the real cause, not the symptom]\n**Fix:** [what changed]\n**Verification:** [commands, tests, or checks that proved it]\n**Prevention:** [what future agents should do differently]\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** [nearby skills or docs updated, or why no general update was needed]\n```\n\nKeep it concrete. Future agents should be able to read the note and avoid the same mistake.\n\n## Update The System When The Lesson Generalizes\n\nIf the bug reveals a reusable rule, update the nearest skill or defaults doc in the same change. Examples:\n\n- debugging lesson -> `systematic-debugging`\n- verification gap -> `verification-before-completion`\n- control-plane or eventing lesson -> `instrumenting-control-planes`\n- React lifecycle or routing lesson -> `building-react-apps`\n- planning omission -> `writing-plans` or `brainstorming`\n\nDo not bury a general lesson only in a project note. The learning note records the case; the skill update changes future behavior.\n\n## Completion Gate\n\nBefore saying a meaningful bug is fixed, check:\n\n1. Is this lesson reusable?\n2. Is there a learning note?\n3. Did any skill or defaults doc need an update?\n4. Did verification prove the fix?\n\nIf the answer to 1 is yes, do not skip the note. If the answer to 3 is no, say why in the note.\n"
  }
]
````

```json
const patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md\n@@\n ## Interactive protocol invariant\n@@\n Related: `docs/architecture/timing-determinism-audit.md` records the May 20,\n@@\n timestamp-only ordering, and silent wake/lease contention.\n+\n+## Interrupted-turn recovery invariant\n+\n+Reattaching a provider session restores conversation context; it does not prove\n+that the interrupted turn is running again. If a persisted active direct worker\n+is missing after a runner restart and the reattached runtime comes back idle\n+without a terminal stop reason or pending user input, recovery must explicitly\n+send a continuation prompt, keep the run `running`, and persist the eventual\n+turn result. The continuation runs as an owned background conversation task so\n+snapshot bootstrap does not block on the agent turn. A newer user turn may\n+supersede it, but that decision must resolve the recovery incident and emit a\n+named event.\n*** Add File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-08-02-reattached-session-is-not-a-resumed-turn.md\n+# A Reattached Session Is Not a Resumed Turn\n+\n+**Date:** 2026-08-02\n+**Context:** OmniHarness direct-worker recovery across production runner restarts; confirmed with Claude Code session `347d62f21fbf`.\n+**Symptom:** Claude worked for a while, then produced no more output until the user asked whether it was stuck. After the nudge, Claude apologized and continued.\n+**Root Cause:** Production restarts at 21:59:40 and 22:07:11 killed the managed agent runtime and its Claude subprocess mid-turn. Automatic recovery successfully spawned Claude with the saved provider session, but treated the resulting idle agent as a completed direct run. Reattaching restored context only; it did not restart inference. The provider transcript recorded a replayed “Continue from where you left off” message, but no assistant tokens or tools followed until the next real user prompt.\n+**Fix:** When an active direct or commit worker is recovered into an idle, non-terminal, non-blocked state, OmniHarness now explicitly sends an authorization-preserving continuation prompt as an owned background worker turn. The run remains `running`; the result is persisted and classified normally. Completion, supersession, and failure emit named events, and failures surface to the user and move the run back to recovery.\n+**Verification:** The regression test failed first because `askAgent` was never called. `pnpm vitest run tests/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.test.ts tests/server/conversations-sync.test.ts` passes with 30 tests. `pnpm typecheck` passes. `pnpm test:lifecycle` passes all 24 files and 33 tests, including the real subprocess restart scenario.\n+**Prevention:** Never equate “provider session accepted” or “runtime agent is idle” with “interrupted work resumed.” Recovery must separately prove process/session restoration, turn execution, result persistence, and terminal classification. Long recovered turns must be tracked background tasks rather than blocking snapshot or reconciliation requests.\n+**Skill/Doc Updates:** Added the interrupted-turn recovery invariant to `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`. No general skill update was needed because the control-plane skill already requires explicit recovery decisions, owned background work, named events, and headless lifecycle tests; the missing rule was specific to this project’s provider-session semantics.\n*** End Patch";
text(await tools.apply_patch(patch));

```

````text
    "text": "---\nname: verification-before-completion\ndescription: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n---\n\n# Verification Before Completion\n\n## Overview\n\nClaiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.\n\n**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.\n\n**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE\n```\n\nIf you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.\n\n## The Gate Function\n\n```\nBEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:\n\n1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?\n2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)\n3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures\n4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?\n   - If NO: State actual status with evidence\n   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence\n5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim\n\nSkip any step = lying, not verifying\n```\n\n## Product-Surface Verification\n\nWhen the work involves an app, UI, or product surface, verification must cover more than whether a single requested feature works.\n\nKeep verification proportional. For tiny mechanical edits, targeted verification is enough: inspect the diff, confirm the changed value/text, and run only the smallest relevant check. Do not escalate to full product-surface verification unless the change can affect behavior, state, navigation, persistence, or the user journey.\n\nAlso verify:\n\n- required functionality is real end-to-end, not a fake component, mock, placeholder, canned response, or fallback path,\n- the primary user stories are actually supported,\n- the primary user journey works end-to-end,\n- any approved agentic user journey tests have been run, or clearly state that they were proposed but not run because approval was not given,\n- return/revisit flows work when they are part of the product,\n- expected empty states are present,\n- expected loading, waiting, or in-progress states are visible,\n- expected error and recovery states are present,\n- expected completed or success states are understandable,\n- expected status-awareness flows are present,\n- the state model is represented correctly,\n- React/server-state invariants are verified when the app uses server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state,\n- persistence behavior for settings, preferences, drafts, and other user-managed state matches the approved design,\n- operational readiness expectations are met for the approved scope,\n- instrumentation and observability exist where the approved scope depends on them,\n- backend or agent workflow decisions emit the planned named events, and a headless/scriptable check can assert the event sequence without relying on UI snapshots,\n- real errors are surfaced rather than silently swallowed or replaced with vague placeholders,\n- onboarding and discoverability work for first-run or empty-state use,\n- risk and trust surfaces are handled well enough to avoid silent failure, ambiguous state, or obvious loss of confidence,\n- when the scope includes a frontend/backend boundary, frontend-visible failures preserve the real error details and stack traces expected by the approved design,\n- baseline expected v1 surfaces and inferred behaviors from the approved story set, spec, or plan are actually present.\n\nWhen the scope includes settings or preferences, also verify:\n\n- each meaningful setting persists or does not persist exactly as designed,\n- the storage location and ownership model match the approved plan,\n- refresh, restart, sign-out, reset, and migration behavior work as intended for the approved scope,\n- settings dialogs avoid redundant section titles, subtitles, explanatory filler, and decorative chrome that does not help the user make a decision.\n\nDo not confuse \"the requested control exists\" with \"the product is usable.\"\n\nFor app, UI, and product-surface work, consider whether an agentic user journey test is the right proof. This mode is approval-gated because it can spend significant tokens: propose it when useful, but only run it if the user explicitly approves.\n\n## Common Failures\n\n| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |\n|-------|----------|----------------|\n| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, \"should pass\" |\n| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |\n| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |\n| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |\n| Meaningful system bug fixed | Learning note plus verification | Fix committed with no durable lesson |\n| React/server state sync works | Stale-response, merge, event-sequence, and ownership tests relevant to the change | UI looked right once |\n| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |\n| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports \"success\" |\n| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |\n| Feature complete | Real end-to-end behavior in the product | Mocked component, canned data, placeholder UI, fallback path |\n\n## Red Flags - STOP\n\n- Using \"should\", \"probably\", \"seems to\"\n- Expressing satisfaction before verification (\"Great!\", \"Perfect!\", \"Done!\", etc.)\n- About to commit/push/PR without verification\n- Trusting agent success reports\n- Accepting \"validated with mocks\" or \"placeholder works\" as completion\n- Relying on partial verification\n- Thinking \"just this once\"\n- Tired and wanting work over\n- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**\n\n## Rationalization Prevention\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Should work now\" | RUN the verification |\n| \"I'm confident\" | Confidence ≠ evidence |\n| \"Just this once\" | No exceptions |\n| \"Linter passed\" | Linter ≠ compiler |\n| \"Agent said success\" | Verify independently |\n| \"I'm tired\" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |\n| \"Partial check is enough\" | Partial proves nothing |\n| \"Different words so rule doesn't apply\" | Spirit over letter |\n\n## Key Patterns\n\n**Tests:**\n```\n✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] \"All tests pass\"\n❌ \"Should pass now\" / \"Looks correct\"\n```\n\n**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**\n```\n✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)\n❌ \"I've written a regression test\" (without red-green verification)\n```\n\n**Build:**\n```\n✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] \"Build passes\"\n❌ \"Linter passed\" (linter doesn't check compilation)\n```\n\n**Requirements:**\n```\n✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion\n❌ \"Tests pass, phase complete\"\n```\n\n**Agent delegation:**\n```\n✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state\n❌ Trust agent report\n```\n\n## Why This Matters\n\nFrom 24 failure memories:\n- your human partner said \"I don't believe you\" - trust broken\n- Undefined functions shipped - would crash\n- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features\n- Time wasted on false completion → redirect → rework\n- Violates: \"Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced.\"\n\n## When To Apply\n\n**ALWAYS before:**\n- ANY variation of success/completion claims\n- ANY expression of satisfaction\n- ANY positive statement about work state\n- Committing, PR creation, task completion\n- Staging, committing, pushing, or opening a PR when the work could include secrets, local environment files, dependencies, caches, build outputs, logs, temporary files, or generated/intermediate artifacts\n- Moving to next task\n- Delegating to agents\n\nWhen a meaningful bug involved architecture, storage, memory, persistence, control planes, background workers, recovery, data loss, security, or a repeated wrong assumption, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before claiming the fix is complete.\n\nWhen a React app has server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state, use `ultrapowers:client-server-state-invariants` to decide which race and synchronization tests are required before claiming completion.\n\nBefore any git publish step, verify `.gitignore` exists for the project and that `git status` does not include secrets, credentials, local environment files, `node_modules`, dependency caches, build outputs, coverage output, logs, temporary files, or intermediate/generated artifacts unless the human explicitly asked to version a specific generated artifact.\n\n**Rule applies to:**\n- Exact phrases\n- Paraphrases and synonyms\n- Implications of success\n- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness\n\n## The Bottom Line\n\n**No shortcuts for verification.**\n\nRun the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.\n\nThis is non-negotiable.\n"

Observability & Resilience Testing\n     2\t\n     3\t> What we keep getting wrong, why our tests don't catch it, and the discipline that fixes it.\n     4\t\n     5\tRelated: `docs/architecture/hot-path-responsiveness-and-resource-leaks.md`\n     6\tdocuments the May 2026 reload/worker-streaming latency incident and the\n     7\tresource-ownership rules for keeping page bootstrap, SSE, and worker stream\n     8\tpaths responsive while agents are running.\n     9\t\n    10\t## Multi-runner client lifecycle\n    11\t\n    12\tEach saved runner profile owns one `RunnerConnection` state machine and at most\n    13\tone main event stream. A registry starts every profile connection, not only the\n    14\tvisible one. Switching the visible workspace changes active managers/query\n    15\tcaches and terminal ownership without closing inactive main streams.\n    16\t\n    17\tEvery bootstrap, snapshot, frame, retry, timer, and mutation checks profile and\n    18\tlearned runner identity before applying state. Stored cursors include the\n    19\trunner's stream epoch. `runner.stopping`, `auth.session_revoked`, and\n    20\t`stream.resync_required` are typed terminal/control outcomes, not generic\n    21\tnetwork failures.\n    22\t\n    23\tElectron, VS Code, and Capacitor host bridges have bounded stream delivery and\n    24\tmust cancel upstream work on overflow. Mobile suspension persists cursors,\n    25\tcloses streams, and reconnects on resume; it must not claim background\n    26\tdelivery. Platform lifecycle contract tests live under `tests/electron`,\n    27\t`tests/vscode`, `tests/mobile`, and `tests/runtime-api`; real two-runner and\n    28\teight-runner journeys live under `tests/e2e`.\n    29\t\n    30\t## Interactive protocol invariant\n    31\t\n    32\tAn advertised interactive agent capability is operational only when the complete user journey is implemented: receive the request, append it to the unified worker stream, render an actionable control in the main conversation, submit the exact protocol response, and append a terminal outcome before resolving the agent request. Side-panel-only controls do not satisfy this invariant. Every form shape and permission option emitted by a supported adapter must be covered by a finite wire-to-UI regression matrix.\n    33\t\n    34\tRelated: `docs/architecture/timing-determinism-audit.md` records the May 20,\n    35\t2026 audit of race-prone frontend and control-plane patterns: cached snapshots\n    36\tpretending to be authoritative, stale async callbacks, timer-owned actions,\n    37\ttimestamp-only ordering, and silent wake/lease contention.\n    38\t\n    39\t## Interrupted-turn recovery invariant\n    40\t\n    41\tReattaching a provider session restores conversation context; it does not prove\n    42\tthat the interrupted turn is running again. If a persisted active direct worker\n    43\tis missing after a runner restart and the reattached runtime comes back idle\n    44\twithout a terminal stop reason or pending user input, recovery must explicitly\n    45\tsend a continuation prompt, keep the run `running`, and persist the eventual\n    46\tturn result. The continuation runs as an owned background conversation task so\n    47\tsnapshot bootstrap does not block on the agent turn. A newer user turn may\n    48\tsupersede it, but that decision must resolve the recovery incident and emit a\n    49\tnamed event.\n    50\t\n    51\t## Why this document exists\n    52\t\n    53\tOver a single afternoon we hit three production-class bugs that the existing test\n    54\tsuite did not catch and could not have caught:\n    55\t\n    56\t1. **Session fails to reconnect after a server restart and gives up after the\n    57\t   first attempt.** No user-visible message, no telemetry event, no recovery\n    58\t   incident — just a silently dead session.\n    59\t2. **`DELETE /conversations/:id` fails with a foreign-key constraint** because\n    60\t   related rows in dependent tables were not cleared first. The error surfaces\n    61\t   as an opaque 500.\n    62\t3. **Running plan-improvement agents fails because leftover state blocks the\n    63\t   new run.** The block is silent; the user sees nothing happen and assumes the\n    64\t   button is broken.\n    65\t\n    66\tThese are not unrelated bugs. They share a single root cause:\n    67\t\n    68\t> **The server makes state transitions that nobody observes.** Neither the user,\n    69\t> nor the UI, nor any test client can tell what the server decided. So bugs in\n    70\t> the *decision* (gave up too early, took the wrong branch, refused silently)\n    71\t> are invisible.\n    72\t\n    73\tThis document records what we learned and the rules we now follow so that this\n    74\tclass of bug stops shipping.\n    75\t\n     1\t# A Reattached Session Is Not a Resumed Turn\n     2\t\n     3\t**Date:** 2026-08-02\n     4\t**Context:** OmniHarness direct-worker recovery across production runner restarts; confirmed with Claude Code session `347d62f21fbf`.\n     5\t**Symptom:** Claude worked for a while, then produced no more output until the user asked whether it was stuck. After the nudge, Claude apologized and continued.\n     6\t**Root Cause:** Production restarts at 21:59:40 and 22:07:11 killed the managed agent runtime and its Claude subprocess mid-turn. Automatic recovery successfully spawned Claude with the saved provider session, but treated the resulting idle agent as a completed direct run. Reattaching restored context only; it did not restart inference. The provider transcript recorded a replayed “Continue from where you left off” message, but no assistant tokens or tools followed until the next real user prompt.\n     7\t**Fix:** When an active direct or commit worker is recovered into an idle, non-terminal, non-blocked state, OmniHarness now explicitly sends an authorization-preserving continuation prompt as an owned background worker turn. The run remains `running`; the result is persisted and classified normally. Completion, supersession, and failure emit named events, and failures surface to the user and move the run back to recovery.\n     8\t**Verification:** The regression test failed first because `askAgent` was never called. `pnpm vitest run tests/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.test.ts tests/server/conversations-sync.test.ts` passes with 30 tests. `pnpm typecheck` passes. `pnpm test:lifecycle` passes all 24 files and 33 tests, including the real subprocess restart scenario.\n     9\t**Prevention:** Never equate “provider session accepted” or “runtime agent is idle” with “interrupted work resumed.” Recovery must separately prove process/session restoration, turn execution, result persistence, and terminal classification. Long recovered turns must be tracked background tasks rather than blocking snapshot or reconciliation requests.\n    10\t**Skill/Doc Updates:** Added the interrupted-turn recovery invariant to `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`. No general skill update was needed because the control-plane skill already requires explicit recovery decisions, owned background work, named events, and headless lifecycle tests; the missing rule was specific to this project’s provider-session semantics.\n"
  }
]
````

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"final","timestamp":"2026-08-02T20:25:23.279Z","phase":"final_answer"} -->
## Codex final · 2026-08-02T20:25:23.279Z

Found it: this was OmniHarness, not Claude.

In session `347d62f21fbf`:

- OmniHarness received production restart requests at 21:59:40 and 22:07:11.
- Each restart killed the managed Claude process mid-turn.
- Recovery restored Claude’s session context but did not restart inference.
- OmniHarness then incorrectly treated the idle reattached worker as finished.
- Your “are you stuck?” message became the next real prompt and woke Claude up.

The regular Claude CLI doesn’t suffer this because its process isn’t owned by OmniHarness’s restart cycle.

I fixed recovery so interrupted direct turns are explicitly continued, remain marked running, persist their result, and emit lifecycle events. See [recovery-reconciler.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.ts:46) and the [regression test](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.test.ts:275).

Verification:

- 30 focused recovery tests passed.
- Type checking passed.
- All 33 lifecycle tests passed, including real process restart recovery.
- OmniHarness was restarted with the fix; runner HTTP returns 200 and the agent runtime is healthy.

One remaining unknown: the logs only record “remote request,” so they don’t identify what issued those two restart requests. The freeze-and-wait behavior itself is fixed.
